\displaystyle\left|\frac{\sqrt{n_{s}}\rho_{\Lambda}^{2}\tilde{\sigma}_{j}\tilde{\sigma}_{j^{\prime}}}{(n+\tau_{\Lambda}^{-2})(n_{s}+\tau_{\Gamma_{s}}^{-2})^{2}}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}}^{\top}\mathbf{\hat{M}}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{\hat{F}_{s}}\mathbf{\hat{F}_{s}}^{\top}\mathbf{\hat{M}}_{s}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{\lambda}_{j^{\prime}}}\right|\asymp\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}}\big|\varepsilon_{\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{c\top}\mathbf{U}_{c}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{c}^{\perp\top}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{c}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{\lambda}_{j^{\prime}}}\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}}.

3.

With probability at least
1-o(1)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\rho_{\Lambda}\rho_{\Gamma_{s}}\tilde{\sigma}_{j}\tilde{\sigma}_{j^{\prime}}\sqrt{n_{s}}}{\sqrt{n+\tau_{\Lambda}^{-2}}(n_{s}+\tau_{\Gamma_{s}}^{-2})^{3/2}}\varepsilon_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{\hat{M}}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{\hat{F}_{s}}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{\gamma}_{sj^{\prime}}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}}\big|\varepsilon_{\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{c\top}\mathbf{U}_{c}^{\perp}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{\gamma}_{sj^{\prime}}}\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}},

and

\left|\frac{\rho_{\Lambda}\rho_{\Gamma_{s}}\tilde{\sigma}_{j}\tilde{\sigma}_{j^{\prime}}\sqrt{n_{s}}}{\sqrt{n+\tau_{\Lambda}^{-2}}(n_{s}+\tau_{\Gamma_{s}}^{-2})^{3/2}}\varepsilon_{j^{\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{\hat{M}}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{\hat{F}_{s}}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{\gamma}_{sj}}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}}.

4.

With probability at least
1-o(1)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\rho_{\Gamma_{s}}^{2}\tilde{\sigma}_{j}\tilde{\sigma}_{j^{\prime}}\sqrt{n_{s}}}{n_{s}+\tau_{\Gamma_{s}}^{-2}}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{\gamma}_{sj}}^{\top}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{\gamma}_{sj^{\prime}}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}}.

∎

A.3
Consistency results under moment assumptions

In this section, we extend the results reported in Theorems
1
and
2
, under more general conditions. In particular, instead of the distributional assumptions on the latent factors and residual noises, we make the following moment based assumptions.

Assumption 12

(Mean-zero noise)
.

For the idiosyncratic noise, we have

E(\mathbf{e}_{si})=0\quad(i=1,\dots,n_{s};s=1,\dots,S).

Assumption 13

(Weak cross-sectional dependence)
.

The total covariance across the
p
columns of the noise is uniformly bounded:

\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{p}\big|\operatorname{Cov}({e}_{sij},\mathbf{e}_{sij^{\prime}})\big|\;\leq\;C_{1},\quad(i=1,\dots,n_{s};s=1,\dots,S),

for some universal constant
C_{1}>0
.

Assumption 14

(Weak across-units dependence)
.

The total covariance of the noise across the samples in each study is uniformly bounded:

\frac{1}{n_{s}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{s}}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n_{s}}\big|\operatorname{Cov}(e_{sij},e_{si^{\prime}j})\big|\;\leq\;C_{2},\quad(j=1,\dots,p;s=1,\dots,S),

for some universal constant
C_{2}>0
.

Assumption 15

(Norm of residual errors)
.

We assume that, with probability at least
1-o(1)
,

\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}p}||\mathbf{E}_{s}\mathbf{e}_{si}||_{F}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}},\quad(i=1,\dots,n_{s})\\
&\frac{1}{n_{s}\sqrt{p}}||\mathbf{e}_{s}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{E}_{s}||_{F}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}},\quad(j=1,\dots,p),\end{aligned}\quad(s=1,\dots,S).

Assumption 16
.

We assume

\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}E\bigg(||\sum_{j=1}^{p}\lambda_{0j}\mathbf{e}_{si}^{(j)}||^{2}+||\sum_{j=1}^{p}\gamma_{0sj}\mathbf{e}_{si}^{(j)}||^{2}\bigg)&\leq M\\
||\mathbf{e}_{si}^{\top}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{0s}||+||\mathbf{e}_{si}^{\top}\mathbf{E}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0s}||&\lesssim n_{s}+p\end{aligned}\qquad(i=1,\dots,n_{s};s=1,\dots,S),

and

\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}E\bigg(||\sum_{i=1}^{n_{s}}\eta_{si}\mathbf{e}_{si}^{(j)}||^{2}+||\sum_{i=1}^{n_{s}}\phi_{si}\mathbf{e}_{si}^{(j)}||^{2}\bigg)&\leq M\\
||\mathbf{e}_{s}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{E}_{s}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0}||+||\mathbf{e}_{s}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{E}_{s}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{0s}||&\lesssim n_{s}+p\end{aligned}\qquad(j=1,\dots,p;s=1,\dots,S).

Assumption 17

(Near orthogonality between latent factors)
.

We assume the latent factors
\mathbf{F}_{0s}
and
\mathbf{M}_{0s}
to be nearly orthogonal, that is, with probability at least
1-o(1)
, we have

||\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{F}_{0s}}\mathbf{M}_{0s}||\lesssim 1,\ \quad(s=1,\dots,S),

where
\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{F}_{0s}}=\mathbf{F}_{0s}\big(\mathbf{F}_{0s}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0s}\big)^{-1}\mathbf{F}_{0s}^{\top}
is the orthogonal projection matrix onto the column space of
\mathbf{F}_{0s}
.

Assumption 18

(Singular values of latent factor matrices)
.

We assume the following conditions for the singular values of the latent factor matrices. With probability at least
1-o(1)
, we have

\big|s_{l}(\mathbf{M}_{0})-\sqrt{n}\big|\lesssim 1,\quad(l=1,\dots,k_{0}),\quad\big|s_{l}(\mathbf{F}_{0s})-\sqrt{n_{s}}\big|\lesssim 1,\quad(l=1,\dots,q_{s};s=1,\dots,S).

Moreover, we assume that with probability at least
1-o(1)
,

\frac{1}{n}\big|\big|\mathbf{M}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{0}-n\mathbf{I}_{k_{0}}\big|\big|\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}},\quad\frac{1}{n_{s}}\big|\big|\mathbf{F}_{0s}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0s}-n_{s}\mathbf{I}_{q_{s}}\big|\big|\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log n_{s}}}{\sqrt{n_{s}}},\quad(s=1,\dots,S).

Theorem 6

(Recovery of latent factors under moment-based assumptions)
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
,
3
–
6
and
12
–
18
hold and
n_{s}=\mathcal{O}(n_{\min}^{2})
, where
n_{\min}=\min_{s=1,\dots,S}n_{s}
, for all
s=1,\dots,S
, then, as
n_{1},\dots,n_{s},p\to\infty
,
with probability at least
1-o(1)
,

\displaystyle\min_{\mathbf{R}_{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{0}\times k_{0}}:\mathbf{R}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{s}=\mathbf{I}_{k_{0}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}}||\mathbf{\hat{M}}_{s}\mathbf{R}_{s}-\mathbf{M}_{0s}||

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}}+\sqrt{\frac{n}{pn_{s}}},

\displaystyle\min_{\mathbf{R}_{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{q_{s}\times q_{s}}:\mathbf{R}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{s}=\mathbf{I}_{q_{s}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}}||\mathbf{\hat{F}_{s}}\mathbf{R}_{s}-\mathbf{F}_{0s}||

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{\min}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}.

Proof of Theorem
6
.

We apply the same steps as in the proof for Theorem
1
. Under Assumptions
3
–
6
and
12
–
17
, with probability at least
1-o(1)
, we have
||\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c}-\mathbf{U}_{0}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{\top}||\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}
, by Proposition
4
, where
\mathbf{U}_{0}
denotes the matrix of left singular vectors of
\mathbf{M}_{0}
. Then, applying Davis-Kahan theorem
(
Davis & Kahan 1970
)
and using Assumption
18
, we obtain the first result. The proof for the second result proceeds by applying Proposition
3
followed by similar steps.
∎

Theorem 7

(Consistency under moment-based assumptions)
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
,
3
–
6
and
12
–
18
hold and
n_{s}=\mathcal{O}(n_{\min}^{2})
, where
n_{\min}=\min_{s=1,\dots,S}n_{s}
, for all
s=1,\dots,S
, then, as
n_{1},\dots,n_{s},p\to\infty
,
with probability at least
1-o(1)
,

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\left|\mathbf{\mu}_{\Lambda}\mathbf{\mu}_{\Lambda}^{\top}-{\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0}{\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0}^{\top}\right|\right|}{\left|\left|{\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0}{\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0}^{\top}\right|\right|}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}},

(36)

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\left|\mathbf{\mu}_{\Gamma_{s}}\mathbf{\mu}_{\Gamma_{s}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{\Gamma}}_{0s}{\mathbf{\Gamma}}_{0s}^{\top}\right|\right|}{\left|\left|{\mathbf{\Gamma}}_{0s}{\mathbf{\Gamma}}_{0s}^{\top}\right|\right|}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log n_{s}}}{\sqrt{n_{s}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}},\quad(s=1,\dots,S).

Proof of Theorem
7
.

The proof mimics the one of Theorem
2
under the novel assumptions. In particular, we follow the same steps as in Theorem
2
, and we prove the first results, using the fact that, with probability at least
1-o(1)
,

1.

||\mathbf{M}_{0}||\asymp\sqrt{n}
by Assumption
18
,

2.

||\mathbf{M}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{0}-n\mathbf{I}_{k_{0}}||\lesssim\sqrt{n\log n}
by Assumption
18
,

3.

||\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\mathbf{M}_{0}||\lesssim 1
, which is implied by Assumption
17
,

4.

||\mathbf{E}||\lesssim\sqrt{n}p^{1/4}+n^{1/4}\sqrt{p}
by Lemma
1
,

5.

||\mathbf{\Delta Y}||\lesssim\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p}
by Lemma
9
,

6.

||\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{0}||\lesssim\sqrt{np}
by Equation (4) of
Bai & Ng (2020)
,

and
||{\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0}||\asymp\sqrt{p}
by Assumption
4
, as well as,
||\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}||=||\mathbf{Q}_{0}^{\perp}||=||\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}||=||\mathbf{N}_{0}^{\perp}||=1
, where
\mathbf{N}_{0}^{\perp}
is defined in (
30
).
Next, use the same bounds, along with
||\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}-\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top}||\leq||\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}-\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top}||\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}
, with probability at least
1-o(1)
, by Proposition
4
, to claim, with probability at least
1-o(1)
,

||\mathbf{\hat{M}}\mathbf{\mu}_{\Lambda}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}_{0}{\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0}^{\top}||\lesssim\sqrt{n}p^{1/4}+n^{1/4}\sqrt{p}.

Finally, we use the fact that, with probability at least
1-o(1)
,

1.

||\mathbf{F}_{0s}||\asymp\sqrt{n_{s}}
by Assumption
18
,

2.

||\mathbf{F}_{0s}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0s}-n_{s}\mathbf{I}_{q_{s}}||\lesssim\sqrt{n_{s}\log n_{s}}
by Assumption
18
,

3.

||\mathbf{E}_{s}||\lesssim\sqrt{n_{s}}p^{1/4}+n_{s}^{1/4}\sqrt{p}
by Lemma
1
,

4.

||\mathbf{Y}_{s}||\lesssim\sqrt{n_{s}p}
by Lemma
4
,

5.

||\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp\top}-\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp\top}||\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}
by Proposition
3
,

together with
\frac{n_{s}}{(n_{s}+\tau_{\Gamma_{s}}^{-2})^{2}}\asymp\frac{1}{n_{s}}
, to prove the second result.
∎

A.4
Additional lemmas

Lemma 1
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
7
, with probability at least
1-o(1)
, we have

||\mathbf{E}_{s}||\lesssim C_{sr,n_{s}}C_{sc,p}(\sqrt{n_{s}}+\sqrt{p}),\quad(s=1,\dots,S),\quad||\mathbf{E}||\lesssim C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}C_{c,p}(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p}).

Replacing Assumptions
2
and
7
with Assumptions
12
–
18
,
with probability at least
1-o(1)
, we have

||\mathbf{E}_{s}||\lesssim\sqrt{n_{s}}p^{1/4}+n_{s}^{1/4}\sqrt{p},\quad(s=1,\dots,S),\quad||\mathbf{E}||\lesssim\sqrt{n}p^{1/4}+n^{1/4}\sqrt{p}.

Proof of Lemma
1
.

The first result follows from
||\mathbf{E}_{s}||=||\mathbf{\Sigma}_{0sr}^{1/2}||||\mathbf{\Sigma}_{0sr}^{-1/2}\mathbf{E}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{0sc}^{-1/2}||||\mathbf{\Sigma}_{0sc}^{1/2}||
and

||\mathbf{\Sigma}_{0sr}^{-1/2}\mathbf{E}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{0sc}^{-1/2}||\lesssim\sqrt{n_{s}}+\sqrt{p}
by Theorem 4.6.1 of
Vershynin (2018)
. The second results is a consequence of
||\mathbf{E}_{s}||\leq||\mathbf{E}_{s}||_{F}\lesssim\sqrt{n_{s}}p^{1/4}+n_{s}^{1/4}\sqrt{p}
, where the second inequality follows from Lemma 1 of
Bai & Ng (2020)
.
∎

Lemma 2
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
–
7
hold. Then, with probability at least
1-o(1)
,

\displaystyle\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{y}_{s}^{(j)}||\lesssim C_{\sigma}C_{sr,n_{s}}\sqrt{n_{s}},\quad(s=1,\dots,S),\quad\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{y}^{(j)}||\lesssim C_{\sigma}C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}\sqrt{n}.

\displaystyle\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{e}_{s}^{(j)}||\lesssim C_{\sigma}C_{sr,n_{s}}\sqrt{n_{s}},\quad(s=1,\dots,S),\quad\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{e}^{(j)}||\lesssim C_{\sigma}C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}\sqrt{n}.

Proof of Lemma
2
.

Consider the following

\displaystyle||\mathbf{y}_{s}^{(j)}||\leq||\mathbf{M}_{0s}||||\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}||+||\mathbf{F}_{0s}||||\mathbf{\gamma}_{0sj}||+||\mathbf{e}_{s}^{(j)}||.

The first result follows from
||\mathbf{M}_{0s}||\asymp||\mathbf{F}_{0s}||\asymp\sqrt{n_{s}}
and
\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{e}_{s}^{(j)}||\lesssim C_{\sigma}C_{sr,n_{s}}\sqrt{n_{s}}
with probability at least
1-o(1)
, by Corollary 5.35 of
Vershynin (2012)
and Lemma 1 of
Laurent & Massart (2000)
, and
\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}||\leq||{\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0}||_{\infty}\sqrt{k_{0}}\asymp 1
,
\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{\gamma}_{0sj}||\leq||{\mathbf{\Gamma}}_{s}||_{\infty}\sqrt{q_{s}}\asymp 1
by Assumptions
4
and
5
.
We derive the analogous result for
y^{(j)}
by noting that
||\mathbf{M}_{0}||\asymp||F_{0}||\asymp\sqrt{n}
and
\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{e}^{(j)}||\lesssim C_{\sigma}C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}\sqrt{n}
with probability at least
1-o(1)
, by Corollary 5.35 of
Vershynin (2012)
and Lemma 1 of
Laurent & Massart (2000)
respectively.
∎

Lemma 3
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
–
7
hold. Then, with probability at least
1-o(1)
,

\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{\hat{y}}_{s}^{c(j)}||\lesssim C_{\sigma}C_{sr,n_{s}}\sqrt{n_{s}},\quad(s=1,\dots,S),\quad\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}||\lesssim C_{\sigma}C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}\sqrt{n}.

Proof of Lemma
3
.

Recall that
\mathbf{\hat{y}}_{s}^{c(j)}=(\mathbf{I}_{n_{s}}-\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp\top})\mathbf{y}_{s}^{(j)}
. The first result follows from

||\mathbf{\hat{y}}_{s}^{c(j)}||=||(\mathbf{I}_{n_{s}}-\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp\top})\mathbf{y}_{s}^{(j)}||\leq||\mathbf{y}_{s}^{(j)}||

and Lemma
2
.
For the second result follows.
∎

Lemma 4
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
–
7
hold. Then, with probability at least
1-o(1)
,

\displaystyle||\mathbf{Y}_{s}||\lesssim\sqrt{n_{s}p},\quad(s=1,\dots,S),\quad||\mathbf{Y}||\lesssim\sqrt{np}.

The same result holds Replacing Assumptions
2
and
7
with Assumptions
12
–
18
.

Proof of Lemma
4
.

First, note
||\mathbf{Y}_{s}||\leq||\mathbf{M}_{0s}{\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0}^{\top}||+||\mathbf{F}_{0s}{\mathbf{\Gamma}}_{s}{0s}^{\top}||+||\mathbf{E}_{s}||
.
By Theorem 4.6.1 of
Vershynin (2018)
, we have
||\mathbf{M}_{0s}||\asymp||\mathbf{F}_{0s}||\asymp\sqrt{n_{s}}
with probability at least
1-o(1)
, and Assumptions
4
and
5
imply
||{\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0}||\asymp||{\mathbf{\Gamma}}_{0s}||\asymp\sqrt{p}
. Therefore, we have
||\mathbf{M}_{0s}{\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0}^{\top}+\mathbf{F}_{0s}{\mathbf{\Gamma}}_{0s}^{\top}||\lesssim\sqrt{n_{s}p}
, with probability at least
1-o(1)
. Lemma
1
provides a probabilistic bound on
||\mathbf{E}_{s}||
.
∎

Lemma 5
.

Let
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}
be the matrix defined in (
31
),
M\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k_{0}}
be a matrix of independent standard Gaussian random variables and
e_{j}
be the
j
-th column of an
n\times p
matrix of zero-mean sub-Gaussian random variables with bounded variances and independent rows.
Then, with probability at least
1-o(1)
, we have

||\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}M||\asymp||M^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}M||\asymp 1,\text{ and }\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}e_{j}||\lesssim\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||e_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}e_{j}||\asymp\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||M^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}e_{j}||\lesssim\log p.

Proof of Lemma
5
.

The results follow from Theorem 4.6.1 of
Vershynin (2018)
and Theorem 2.1 of
Rudelson & Vershynin (2013)
.
∎

Lemma 6
.

Let
\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k_{0}}
be the matrix of left singular vectors associated to the
k_{0}
leading singular values of
\mathbf{M}_{0}
. Then, we have

\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}\mathbf{e}^{(j)}||\lesssim\log p

with probability at least
1-o(1)
.

Proof of Lemma
6
.

The result follows from Theorem 2.1 of
Rudelson & Vershynin (2013)
.
∎

Lemma 7
.

Let
\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}=\big[\mathbf{U}_{01}^{c\top}~\cdots~\mathbf{U}_{0S}^{c\top}\big]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k_{0}}
, where
\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{c}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{s}\times k_{0}}
is the matrix of left singular vectors associated to the
k_{0}
leading singular values of
\mathbf{M}_{0}
and
\mathbf{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{s}\times q_{s}}
is a matrix with independent standard Gaussian entries. Then, with probability at least
1-o(1)
, we have

||\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{c}||\lesssim 1.

Proof of Lemma
7
.

Note
\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{c}\sim MN_{q_{s},k_{0}}(0,\mathbf{I}_{q_{s}},\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{c\top}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{c})
. Recall that
\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}=\mathbf{I}_{k_{0}}
and
\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{c\top}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{c}=\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}-\mathbf{U}_{0-s}^{c\top}\mathbf{U}_{0-s}^{c}
, where
\mathbf{U}_{0-s}
denotes the matrix obtained by removing the block
\mathbf{U}_{0s}
from
\mathbf{U}_{0}
, which implies
\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{c\top}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{c}\preceq\mathbf{I}_{k_{0}}
and, consequently, by Theorem 4.6.1 of
Vershynin (2018)
,
||\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{c}||\lesssim\sqrt{k_{0}}+\sqrt{q_{s}}
with probability
1-o(1)
.
∎

Lemma 8
.

Let
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times q}
be such that
U^{\top}U=\mathbf{I}_{q}
, and
M\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
be a matrix with independent standard Gaussian entries. Then, with probability at least
1-o(1)
, we have

||M^{\top}U||\lesssim\sqrt{k}+\sqrt{p}.

Proof of Lemma
8
.

It is enough to note that
M^{\top}U\sim MN_{k,q}(0,\mathbf{I}_{k},\mathbf{I}_{q})
and apply Theorem 4.6.1 of
Vershynin (2018)
.
∎

Lemma 9
.

Let
\Delta\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be the matrix defined in (
32
), then under Assumptions
1
–
7
, we have

\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}||\lesssim C_{\sigma}C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{\min}}}+\frac{\sqrt{n_{\max}}}{p}\big),

||\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{Y}||\lesssim(C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}C_{c,p})^{2}\big(\frac{\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{n_{\min}}}+\frac{\sqrt{n_{\max}}}{\sqrt{p}}\big),

and

\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{y}^{(j)}||\lesssim C_{\sigma}^{2}C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}^{4}C_{c,p}^{2}\big(1+\frac{n_{\max}}{p}\big),

with probability at least
1-o(1)
, where
n_{\max}=\max_{s=1,\dots,S}n_{s}
and
n_{\min}=\min_{s=1,\dots,S}n_{s}
.
Replacing Assumptions
2
and
7
with Assumptions
12
–
18
,
with probability at least
1-o(1)
, we have

||\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{Y}||\lesssim\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p}.

Proof of Lemma
9
.

First, note that

\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}=\begin{bmatrix}(\mathbf{U}_{01}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{01}^{\perp\top}-\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\perp\top})\mathbf{y}_{1}^{(j)}\\
\vdots\\
(\mathbf{U}_{0S}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{0S}^{\perp\top}-\mathbf{U}_{S}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{S}^{\perp\top})\mathbf{y}_{S}^{(j)}\end{bmatrix}

where
\mathbf{y}_{s}^{(j)}
denotes the
j
-th column of
\mathbf{Y}_{s}
.
Note that
||(\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp\top}-\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp\top})\mathbf{y}_{s}^{(j)}||\lesssim||\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp\top}-\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp\top}||||\mathbf{y}_{s}^{(j)}||\lesssim(C_{sr,n_{s}}C_{sc,p})^{2}\big(\frac{1}{n_{s}}+\frac{1}{p}\big)C_{\sigma}C_{sr,n_{s}}\sqrt{n_{s}}=C_{\sigma}C_{sr,n_{s}}^{3}C_{sc,p}^{2}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{s}}}+\frac{\sqrt{n_{s}}}{p}\big)
. Thus,
||\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}||\lesssim C_{\sigma}C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}^{3}C_{c,p}^{2}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{\min}}}+\frac{\sqrt{n_{\max}}}{p}\big)
, with probability at least
1-o(1)
, where
n_{\max}=\max_{s=1,\dots,S}n_{s}
and
n_{\min}=\min_{s=1,\dots,S}n_{s}
, since
\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{y}_{s}^{(j)}||\lesssim C_{\sigma}C_{sr,n_{s}}\sqrt{n_{s}}
with probability at least
1-o(1)
, by Lemma
2
.
For the second result, consider

\mathbf{\Delta Y}=\begin{bmatrix}(\mathbf{U}_{01}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{01}^{\perp\top}-\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\perp\top})\mathbf{Y}_{1}\\
\vdots\\
(\mathbf{U}_{0S}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{0S}^{\perp\top}-\mathbf{U}_{S}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{S}^{\perp\top})\mathbf{Y}_{S}\end{bmatrix}

with

||(\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp\top}-\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp\top})\mathbf{Y}_{s}||\lesssim||\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp\top}-\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp\top}||||\mathbf{Y}_{s}||.

Applying the bounds in Proposition
3
and Lemma
4
with

||\mathbf{\Delta Y}||\lesssim S\max_{s=1,\dots,S}||(\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp\top}-\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp\top})\mathbf{Y}_{s}||

proves the result.
Finally, note
\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{y}^{(j)}=\sum_{s=1}^{S}\mathbf{e}_{s}^{(j)\top}(\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp\top}-\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp\top})\mathbf{y}_{s}^{(j)}
, and
|\mathbf{e}_{s}^{(j)\top}(\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp\top}-\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp\top})\mathbf{y}_{s}^{(j)}|\lesssim||\mathbf{e}_{s}^{(j)}||||\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{0s}^{\perp\top}-\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{s}^{\perp\top}||||\mathbf{y}_{s}^{(j)}||
. The bounds in Proposition
3
and Lemma
2
complete the proof.

∎

Lemma 10
.

Let
\mathbf{r}^{(j)}=\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)})
, where
\Delta
and
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}
are defined in (
32
) and (
31
) respectively.
Then, under Assumption
1
–
7
, we have

||\mathbf{r}^{(j)}||\lesssim C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{\min}}}+\frac{\sqrt{n_{\max}}}{p}\bigg)+\log p,

with probability at least
1-o(1)
, where
n_{\max}=\max_{s=1,\dots,S}n_{s}
and
n_{\min}=\min_{s=1,\dots,S}n_{s}
.

Proof of Lemma
10
.

First, consider

||\mathbf{r}^{(j)}||\leq||\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\mathbf{M}_{0}^{\top}||||\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}||+||\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\mathbf{e}^{(j)}||+||\Delta\mathbf{y}^{c(j)}||.

Moreover,
||\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\mathbf{M}_{0}^{\top}||\lesssim 1
,
||\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\mathbf{e}^{(j)}||\lesssim\log p
and
||\Delta y^{c(j)}||\lesssim C_{\sigma}C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{\min}}}+\frac{\sqrt{n_{\max}}}{p})
with probability at least
1-o(1)
by Lemma
5
and Lemma
9
and

||\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}||\lesssim||{\mathbf{\Lambda}}||_{\infty}\sqrt{k_{0}}\asymp 1
by Assumption
4
.
∎

Lemma 11
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
–
7
hold,
n_{s}=\mathcal{O}(n_{\min}^{2})
, where
n_{\min}=\min_{s=1,\dots,S}n_{s}
, for all
s=1,\dots,S
, and
\sqrt{n}/p=o(1)
. Then, as
n_{1},\dots,n_{S},p\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{\mu}_{\lambda_{j}}^{\top}\mathbf{\mu}_{\lambda_{j^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\overset{pr}{\to}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}^{\top}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}||(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top})\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}||^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{pr}{\to}\sigma_{0j}^{2}.

Proof of Lemma
11
.

The first result follows easily from the proof of Theorem
3
. For the second result, note

\displaystyle\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)\top}\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}=

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\right)^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\right)\left(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\right)+\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)\top}\mathbf{\Delta}^{2}\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}

\displaystyle+2\left(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\right)^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}.

Moreover,

\left(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\right)^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\right)\left(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\right)\sim\chi_{n}+\psi_{n},

with
\chi_{n}\sim||\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}||^{2}\chi_{n-\sum_{s}q_{s}}^{2}
,
E[\psi_{n}]=(n-k)\sigma_{0j}^{2}
,
V(\psi_{n})\asymp n
, and
\chi_{n}
being independent of
\psi_{n}
, while
y^{c(j)\top}\mathbf{\Delta}^{2}\hat{y}^{c(j)}\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{p}
and
\left(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\right)^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}\asymp 1
with probability at least
1-o(1)
. Hence,

\frac{1}{n}\left(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\right)^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\right)\left(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\right)\overset{pr}{\to}\sigma_{0j}^{2}+||\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}||^{2},

by the weak law of large numbers and
\frac{n}{n-\sum_{s}q_{s}}\asymp 1
, which in combination with

\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top}\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j^{\prime})}\overset{pr}{\to}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}^{\top}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j^{\prime}}
, proves the result.
∎

Lemma 12
.

Under Assumptions
1
–
7
,
n_{s}=\mathcal{O}(n_{\min}^{2})
, where
n_{\min}=\min_{s=1,\dots,S}n_{s}
, for all
s=1,\dots,S
, and
\sqrt{n}/p=o(1)
. Then, as
n_{1},\dots,n_{S},p\to\infty
, we have

\mathbf{\mu}_{\gamma_{sj}}^{\top}\mathbf{\mu}_{\gamma_{sj^{\prime}}}\overset{pr}{\to}\mathbf{\gamma}_{0sj}^{\top}\mathbf{\gamma}_{0sj^{\prime}}.

Proof.

It follows from Theorem
3
.
∎

Lemma 13
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
–
7
hold. Then,

\tilde{\Pi}\big(\max_{j=1,\dots,p}\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}\leq C_{\tilde{\sigma}}\big)=1-o(1)

with probability at least
1-o(1)
, where
C_{\tilde{\sigma}}<\infty
is a finite constant.

Proof of Lemma
13
.

Recall that
\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}\sim IG\big(\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}/2,\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}\delta_{j}^{2}/2\big)
, where
\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}=\nu_{0}+n
and
\delta_{j}^{2}=\frac{1}{\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}}\big(\nu_{0}\sigma_{0}^{2}+\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)\top}\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}-\frac{1}{n+\tau_{\Lambda}^{-2}}\mathbf{\mu}_{\lambda_{j}}\mathbf{\mu}_{\lambda_{j}}\big).

Recall that
\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}=\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)}+\mathbf{r}^{(j)}
, where
\mathbf{r}^{(j)}=\Delta y^{(j)}-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)})
, and
\Delta
and
\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}
are defined in (
32
) and (
31
), respectively.
Note that
||\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)}||^{2}=||\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}||^{2}+||\mathbf{e}^{(j)}||^{2}
and
||\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}||^{2}\sim\sum_{s=1}^{S}||\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}||^{2}\chi_{n_{s}}^{2}
. Applying Proposition 5.16 of
Vershynin (2012)
, we have
||\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}||^{2}\lesssim n
with probability at least
1-o(1)
. Moreover,
||\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{(j)}||^{2}\lesssim||\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)}||^{2}+||\mathbf{r}^{(j)}||^{2}\lesssim n
, since
||\mathbf{r}^{(j)}||^{2}\lesssim 1
. Therefore,
\mathbf{\gamma}_{s}\delta_{j}^{2}\lesssim||\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}||^{2}+n||\mathbf{\mu}_{\lambda_{j}}||^{2}\lesssim n
, since
n||\mathbf{\mu}_{\lambda_{j}}||^{2}\lesssim||\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}||^{2}
by
Theorem 5 of
Zhang & Zou (2014)
with
v_{n}\asymp n
concludes the proof. ∎

Lemma 14
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
–
7
hold,
n_{s}=\mathcal{O}(n_{\min}^{2})
, where
n_{\min}=\min_{s=1,\dots,S}n_{s}
, for all
s=1,\dots,S
, and
\sqrt{n}/p=o(1)
. Then, as
n_{1},\dots,n_{S},p\to\infty
, we have

\max_{j=1,\dots,p}|\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\sigma_{0j}|\lesssim\big(\frac{\log{p}}{n}\big)^{1/3}+\frac{1}{p}

with probability at least
1-o(1)
, where
\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}
is a sample for the
j
-th error variance from
\tilde{\Pi}
.

Proof of Lemma
14
.

Recall that
\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}\sim IG(\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}/2,\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}\delta_{j}^{2}/2)
. First consider

\displaystyle\delta_{j}^{2}=\frac{1}{\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}}\bigg\{\nu_{0}\sigma_{0}^{2}+\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)\top}\Big(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\frac{n}{n+\tau_{\Lambda}^{-2}}\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top}\Big)y^{c(j)}\bigg\}

and recall from the proof of Proposition
4
that
\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}=(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp})(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)})+\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}
.
In the following, we show

\delta_{j}^{2}=\frac{C_{j}^{2}}{n}+R_{j}

(37)

and
C_{j}^{2}=(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)})^{\top}\big(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}\big)(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)})
, with
E[C_{j}^{2}]=(n-k_{0})\sigma_{0j}^{2}
and
C_{j}^{2}
admitting the representation as the sum of
n-k_{0}
of squares of independent zero-mean sub-Gaussian random variables,
and
\max_{j=1,\dots,p}|R_{j}|\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{p}
, with probability at least
1-o(1)
,
where

\begin{aligned} R_{j}=&\frac{1}{\nu_{n}}\Bigg[\nu_{0}\sigma_{0}^{2}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})\{-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)})+\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}\}\\
&+\{-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)})+\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}\}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\\
&+\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)\top}\big\{(\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}-\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top})+\big(1-\frac{n}{n+\tau_{\Lambda}^{-2}}\big)\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top}\big\}\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}\Bigg]\\
&+\big(\frac{1}{\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}}-\frac{1}{n}\big)\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\end{aligned}.

Note

\displaystyle\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)\top}\big(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\frac{n}{n+\tau_{\Lambda}^{-2}}\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top}\big)y^{c(j)}=

\displaystyle\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)\top}(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})y^{c(j)}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)\top}\big[(\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}-\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top})+\big(1-\frac{n}{n+\tau_{\Lambda}^{-2}}\big)\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top}\big]\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}.

First, we analyze
\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)\top}(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)\top}(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}=

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})\mathbf{e}^{(j)}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})\{-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)})+\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}\}

\displaystyle+\{-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)})+\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}\}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})\mathbf{e}^{(j)}

since
(I-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})\mathbf{M}_{0}=0
.
Note that
\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\sim\sigma_{0j}^{2}\chi_{n-k_{0}}^{2}
, while

\displaystyle\max_{j=1,\dots,p}|\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})

\displaystyle\{-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)})+\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}\}|

\displaystyle\lesssim||\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}[\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)})+\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}]||

\displaystyle+||\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}||||\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}||||-\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)})+\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}||

\displaystyle\lesssim||\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\mathbf{M}_{0}||||\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}||+||\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\mathbf{e}^{(j)}||+||\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}||

\displaystyle+||\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}||||\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}||(||\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}(\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{\lambda}_{0j}+\mathbf{e}^{(j)})||+||\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}||)

\displaystyle\lesssim\log p+(C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}C_{c,p})^{4}\frac{n_{\max}}{p},

since, with probability at least
1-o(1)
, we have

\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\mathbf{e}^{(j)}||\lesssim\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\mathbf{M}_{0}||\asymp\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\mathbf{e}^{(j)}||\lesssim\log p

and

||\mathbf{P}_{0}^{\perp}\mathbf{M}_{0}||\asymp 1,

by Lemma
5
, and
\max_{j=1,\dots,p}|\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}|\leq C_{\sigma}^{2}C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}^{4}C_{c,p}^{2}\big(1+\frac{n_{\max}}{p}\big)
,
\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{\Delta}y^{(j)}||\asymp C_{\sigma}C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{\min}}}+\frac{\sqrt{n_{\max}}}{p}\big)
, where
n_{\max}=\max_{s=1,\dots,S}n_{s}
and
n_{\min}=\min_{s=1,\dots,S}n_{s}
, by Lemma
9
, and
||\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}||=1
. Also,

\displaystyle\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)\top}\big\{(\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}-\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top})

\displaystyle+\big(1-\frac{n}{n+\tau_{\Lambda}^{-2}}\big)\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top}\big\}\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}

\displaystyle\lesssim||\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}||^{2}\big(||\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}-\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top}||+\frac{\tau_{\Lambda}^{2}}{n+\tau_{\Lambda}^{-2}}||\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top}||\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim(C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}C_{c,p})^{2}\big(1+\frac{n}{p}\big),

since, with probability at least
1-o(1)
, we have
\max_{j=1,\dots,p}||\mathbf{\hat{y}}^{c(j)}||\lesssim\sqrt{n}
and
||\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top}-\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top}||\lesssim(C_{r,\{n_{s}\}_{s}^{S}}C_{c,p})^{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{p}\big))
by Lemma
3
and Proposition
4
, and
||\mathbf{U}^{c}\mathbf{U}^{c\top}||=1
.
Finally, note that
\big(\frac{1}{\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}}-\frac{1}{n}\big)\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})\mathbf{e}^{(j)}\lesssim\frac{1}{n}
, with probability at least
1-o(1)
, since
\frac{1}{\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}}+\frac{1}{n}\asymp\frac{1}{n^{2}}
, and
\max_{j=1,\dots,p}|\mathbf{e}^{(j)\top}(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{c\top})\mathbf{e}^{(j)}-\sigma_{0j}^{2}|\lesssim\sqrt{n}
, with probability at least
1-o(1)
, by Proposition 5.16 of
Vershynin (2012)
. The fact that
\mathbf{\gamma}_{s}\asymp n
proves
\max_{j=1,\dots,p}|R_{j}|\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{p}
, with probability at least
1-o(1)
.
Next, we show
\max_{j=1,\dots,p}|\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\sigma_{0j}^{2}|
decreases to
0
as
n
and
p
diverge, following the same steps as in the proof for Theorem 3.6 of
Chattopadhyay et al. (2024)
.
Let
U_{j}=\frac{\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}\delta_{j}^{2}}{2}\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{-2}-\frac{\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}}{2}
and write
\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\sigma_{0j}^{2}
as

\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\sigma_{0j}=\big(1+\frac{2}{\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}}U_{j}\big)^{-1}\big(\delta_{j}^{2}-\sigma_{0j}^{2}-\frac{2}{\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}}U_{j}\sigma_{0j}^{2}\big).

By Lemma E.7 in
Chattopadhyay et al. (2024)
, we have
\max_{j=1,\dots,p}|U_{j}|/\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}\lesssim(\log{p}/n)^{1/3}
with probability at least
1-o(1)
. Thus,
\min_{j=1,\dots,p}\big|1+\frac{2}{\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}}U_{j}\big|\gtrsim 1/2
with probability at least
1-o(1)
. Hence,

\max_{j=1,\dots,p}|\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\sigma_{0j}^{2}|\lesssim\max_{j=1,\dots,p}|\delta_{j}^{2}-\sigma_{0j}^{2}|+\sigma_{0j}^{2}\max_{j=1,\dots,p}\frac{|U_{j}|}{\mathbf{\gamma}_{n}}.

From the representation in (
37
), we have

\delta_{j}^{2}-\sigma_{0j}^{2}=\frac{\sigma_{0j}^{2}}{n}\bigg\{\frac{C_{j}}{\sigma_{0j}^{2}}-(n-k)\bigg\}-\frac{k}{n}\sigma_{0j}^{2}+R_{j},

and Lemma E.7 of
Chattopadhyay et al. (2024)
gives

\max_{j=1,\dots,p}\bigg|\frac{C_{j}}{\sigma_{0j}^{2}}-(n-k)\bigg|\lesssim n\big(\frac{\log{p}}{n}\big)^{1/3},

with probability at least
1-o(1)
. Combining all of the above, we get

\max_{j=1,\dots,p}|\tilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\sigma_{0j}|\lesssim\big(\frac{\log{p}}{n}\big)^{1/3}+\frac{1}{p},

with probability at least
1-o(1)
.
∎

Appendix B
Extensions to the heteroscedastic case

We present an extension of the methodology presented in the paper to the heteroscedastic design.
In such a case, the conditional conjugacy on the loading matrices seen in the homoscedastic is lost. Hence, instead of simply sampling sequentially
{\mathbf{\Lambda}}
and then each
{\mathbf{\Gamma}}_{s}
given
\mathbf{\Lambda}
, it becomes necessary to alternate between the shared and specific loadings within a Gibbs sampler.
For the study-specific loadings and variances we specify conjugate Normal-Inverse Gamma priors

\begin{aligned} \mathbf{\gamma}_{sj}&\mid\sigma_{sj}^{2}\sim N_{q_{s}}\left(0,\tau_{\Gamma_{s}}\sigma_{sj}^{2}\right),\quad\sigma_{sj}^{2}\sim IG\left(\frac{\nu_{0}}{2},\frac{\nu_{0}\sigma_{0}^{2}}{2}\right)\\
\end{aligned}\quad j=1,\dots,p,\quad s=1,\dots,S

and
assign semi-conjugate Normal inverse gamma priors

\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}\mid\tau_{\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}}^{2}\sim N_{k_{0}}\left(0,\tau_{\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}}^{2}\right),\quad\tau_{\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}}^{2}\sim IG(\nu/2,\nu/2\tau_{0}^{2}).

After estimating the latent factors as in Section
2.2
, the posterior computation for the loadings can be performed using the following Gibbs sampler.

1.

Given the previous sample for
{\mathbf{\Lambda}}
, for
s=1,\dots,S
, define
\mathbf{\tilde{Y}_{s}}=\mathbf{Y}_{s}-\mathbf{M_{s}}{\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\top}
and sample
\{(\mathbf{\gamma}_{sj},\sigma_{sj}^{2})\}_{j=1}^{p}
from their conditional
NIG
posterior
(\mathbf{\gamma}_{sj},\sigma_{sj}^{2})
as

(\mathbf{\gamma}_{sj},\sigma_{sj}^{2})\mid\mathbf{\tilde{Y}_{s}}\sim NIG\left(\mathbf{\mu}_{\gamma_{sj}},\mathbf{K}_{j},\nu_{n_{s}},\nu_{n_{s}}\Delta_{sj}^{2}\right),\quad j=1,\dots,p,

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{\mu}_{\gamma_{sj}}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{\hat{F}_{s}}^{\top}\mathbf{\hat{F}_{s}}+\frac{\mathbf{I}_{q_{s}}}{\tau_{\mathbf{\gamma}_{sj}}^{2}}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{\hat{F}_{s}}^{\top}\mathbf{\tilde{y}}_{s}^{(j)},

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{j}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{\hat{F}_{s}}^{\top}\mathbf{\hat{F}_{s}}+\frac{\mathbf{I}_{q_{s}}}{\tau_{\mathbf{\gamma}_{sj}}^{2}}\right)^{-1},

\displaystyle\nu_{n_{s}}

\displaystyle=\nu_{0}+n_{s},

\displaystyle\nu_{n_{s}}\Delta_{sj}^{2}

\displaystyle=\nu_{0}\Delta_{0}^{2}+\left(||\mathbf{\tilde{y}}_{s}^{(j)}||^{2}-\mathbf{\mu}_{\gamma_{sj}}^{\top}K_{j}^{-1}\mathbf{\mu}_{\gamma_{sj}}\right),

and
\mathbf{\tilde{y}}_{s}^{(j)}
is the
j
-th column of
\mathbf{\tilde{Y}_{s}}

2.

Given the samples for
\{{\mathbf{\Gamma}}_{s}\}_{s=1}^{S}
, sample
\{(\mathbf{\lambda}_{j},\tau_{\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}}^{2})\}_{j=1}^{p}
from its full conditional. In particular, for
j=1,\dots,p
, define
\mathbf{\bar{Y}}=\big[\mathbf{\bar{Y}}_{s}^{\top}~\cdots~\mathbf{\bar{Y}}_{S}^{\top}\big]^{\top}
, where
\mathbf{\bar{Y}}_{s}=\mathbf{Y}_{s}-\mathbf{\hat{F}_{s}}{\mathbf{\Gamma}}_{s}
, then

(a)

Sample
\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}
from its full conditional

\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}\mid\bar{y}^{(j)},\tau_{\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}}^{2}\sim N_{k}(\mathbf{\mu}_{\lambda_{j}},V_{j}),

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{\mu}_{\lambda_{j}}

\displaystyle=V_{j}^{-1}\mathbf{\hat{M}}^{\top}{D_{j}}^{-1}\mathbf{\bar{y}}^{(j)},

\displaystyle V_{j}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{\hat{M}}^{\top}{D_{j}}^{-1}\mathbf{\hat{M}}+\frac{\mathbf{I}_{k_{0}}}{\tau_{\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}}^{2}}\right)^{-1},

\displaystyle{D_{j}}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\sigma_{j1}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{n_{1}}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&\sigma_{j2}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{n_{2}}&&0\\
\vdots\\
0&\cdots&0&\sigma_{sj}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{n_{s}}\end{bmatrix},

and
\mathbf{\bar{y}}^{(j)}
is the
j
-th column of
\mathbf{\bar{Y}}
.

(b)

Sample
\tau_{\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}}^{2}
from its full conditional

\tau_{\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}}^{2}\mid\mathbf{\bar{y}}^{(j)},{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\sim IG\left(\frac{\nu_{0}+n}{2},\frac{\nu_{0}\tau_{0}^{2}+||\mathbf{\bar{y}}^{(j)}-\mathbf{\hat{M}}\mathbf{\lambda}_{j}||^{2}}{2}\right).

We expect this method to still suffer from mild undercoverage. A strategy similar to the one devised in Section
2.5
would be necessary to ensure the frequentist validity of coverage of credible intervals.

Appendix C
Details about the estimation of the latent dimensions

For the study
s
and each
k
, we let
\mathbf{\hat{M}}_{s}^{(k)}
be the matrix of left singular vectors of
\mathbf{Y}_{s}
scaled by
\sqrt{n_{s}}
and let
\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}_{s}^{(k)}=\frac{1}{n+1/\tau_{s}^{2}}\mathbf{Y}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{\hat{M}}_{s}^{(k)}
be the conditional posterior mean for the loadings, where
\tau_{s}
is chosen as in Section
2.6
. We estimate the residual error variances as
\sigma_{sj}^{(k)2}=\frac{1}{n_{s}}||(\mathbf{I}_{n_{s}}-\mathbf{\hat{M}}_{s}^{(k)}\mathbf{\hat{M}}_{s}^{(k)\top}/n_{s})\mathbf{y}_{s}^{(j)}||_{F}^{2}.
Finally, we approximate the likelihood computed at the joint likelihood estimate as

\displaystyle l_{sk}\approx\hat{l}_{sk}

\displaystyle=l(\mathbf{\hat{M}}_{s}^{(k)},\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}_{s}^{(k)},\mathbf{\hat{\Sigma}}_{s}^{(k)};\mathbf{Y}_{s})

\displaystyle=-\frac{n_{s}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\log|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{s}^{(k)}|-\frac{1}{2}tr\big\{(\mathbf{Y}_{s}-\mathbf{\hat{M}}_{s}^{(k)}\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}_{s}^{(k)\top})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{s}^{(k)-1}(\mathbf{Y}_{s}-\mathbf{\hat{M}}_{s}^{(k)}\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}_{s}^{(k)\top})\big\},

where
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{s}^{(k)}=\text{diag}(\sigma_{s1}^{(k)2},\dots,\sigma_{sp}^{(k)2})
.

Appendix D
Additional details about the numerical experiments

We report additional details about the numerical experiments presented in Section
4
of the main article. For
SVI
, we use a batch size of
20\%
of the sample size. All other hyperparameters are set to default values. For all methods, we take
\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}^{\top}
and
\mathbf{\hat{\Gamma}}_{s}\mathbf{\hat{\Gamma}}_{s}^{\top}
as estimators for
\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\top}
and
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{s}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{s}^{\top}
where
\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}
and
\mathbf{\hat{\Gamma}}_{s}
are the posterior mean for
\mathbf{\Lambda}
and
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{s}
, respectively.
We also considered alternative strategies, such as using posterior means for
\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\top}
and
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{s}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{s}^{\top}
, but we noticed negligible differences. To estimate frequentist coverage of credible intervals, we obtained 500 posterior samples from the posterior distribution and computed the entry-wise equal-tail credible intervals. To implement the two variational inference algorithms from
Hansen et al. (2024)
, we use code available at
https://github.com/blhansen/VI-MSFA
. Code for implementing BLAST and replicating
results is available at
https://github.com/maurilorenzo/BLAST
.
To compare computational costs, we performed the experiments on a Laptop with 11th Gen Intel(R) Core(TM) i7-1165G7 2.80 GHz and 16GB RAM.

Appendix E
Additional experiments

First, we report in Tables
9
and
10
the results on the heteroscedastic case of the first experiment presented in Section
4
.
There are no significant differences in conclusions compared with the homoscedastic case.
BLAST
and
SPECTRAL
are the best performing methods, with
BLAST
(
SPECTRAL
) having better accuracy when
p=500
(
p=5000
respectively). Table
10
provides additional support for
BLAST
in terms of providing well-calibrated credible intervals.

We also performed an additional experiment in the homescedastic case in a lower dimensional scenario. In particular, we adopted the same setting as for the first experiment in Section
4
with
k_{0}=5
and
q_{s}=4
and let
S=3
,
n_{s}=300
, and
p=200
. We also compare to the maximum likelihood estimate of the multi-study factor model obtained via the expectation maximization algorithm
(
De Vito et al. 2019
)
(
EM
, henceforth) and a Bayesian estimate performing Bayesian computation via a Gibbs sampler
(
De Vito et al. 2021
)
(
GIBBS
, henceforth), using the code available at
https://github.com/rdevito/MSFA
.
For
EM
, we estimate latent factors via the Thomson’s factor score, which corresponds to their conditional mean given the estimates for factor loadings and residuals variances, and we take
\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}^{\top}
and
\mathbf{\hat{\Gamma}}_{s}\mathbf{\hat{\Gamma}}_{s}^{\top}
as estimates for
\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\top}
and
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{s}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{s}^{\top}
where
\mathbf{\hat{\Lambda}}
and
\mathbf{\hat{\Gamma}}_{s}
are the maximum likelihood estimates for
\mathbf{\Lambda}
and
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{s}
respectively.
For
GIBBS
, we obtain point estimates for latent factors by averaging the posterior samples after alignment using the method of
Poworoznek et al. (2025)
, and estimate
\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\top}
and
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{s}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{s}^{\top}
via their posterior mean. We run the Gibbs sampler for 20000 iterations, discarding the first 10000 as burn in, and retain one sample every 20, resulting in a total of 500 thinned posterior samples.
For
BLAST
, we set
\tau=0.2
.

Table
11
reports a comparison in terms of estimation accuracy and frequentist coverage of
95\%
credible intervals. The maximum likelihood estimate (
EM
) and
SVI
achieve the best estimation accuracy.
BLAST
estimation accuracy is slightly worse but provides more precise uncertainty quantification than all competitors considered.
Moreover,
BLAST
and
SPECTRAL
are much faster than all the alternatives (about
10
times faster than
CAVI
and
150
times faster than
SVI
) (see Table
12
). Hence, even if
BLAST
was mainly motivated by high-dimensional applications, it still performs competitively in lower dimensional examples.

95\%
credible intervals in the additional experiment. Values have been multiplied by
10^{2}
. We report the mean and standard error over 50 replications.

Appendix F
Additional details and figures for the application to gene association among immune cells data

As pre-processing, we normalize the data by applying the inverse CDF transform estimating the marginal CDFs via their empirical counterpart and we standardize the variables to have zero mean and unit standard deviation. To apply the criterion in (
11
), we set
\tau=0.2
.

We report some results from fitting our
BLAST
approach to data on
p=2846
genes.
Figure
1
shows the reconstructed within-study correlation matrices (left and middle panels) and rescaled shared variances for 1000 genes. All elements whose 95% credible intervals for the correlation included zero were set to zero in the plot. There are clear similarities in the correlation structure across the three studies. However, non-negligible differences in the strength of the signal are present, with study GSE109125, which is the study where all the methods had the lowest out-of-sample performance, having the weakest signal. A clear shared low-rank structure arises, which is present but less visible in the reconstructed covariance for each study.

Figure 1
:
Reconstructed within-study
correlation matrices (left and middle panels) and rescaled shared component (right panel) for 1000 genes. Elements for which the
95\%
credible intervals included
0
were set to
0
.

Next, we study the correlation induced by the estimated shared covariance matrix
\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{\Lambda}+\mathbf{\Sigma}
. Among the 1000 genes in Figure
1
, we select
392
“hub” genes that have absolute correlations greater than
0.5
with at least 20 other genes. Focusing on these hub genes, we show the dependence network in Figure
2
, where the size of each gene (i.e. node) is proportional to the number of connections. We identify four main clusters of genes using the Louvain method
(
Clauset et al. 2004
)
.
The first cluster (shown in green) is characterized by a strong representation of cell cycle and DNA replication genes, such as
Dhfr
,
Chaf1b
,
Aspm
, and
Aurkb
. In the second cluster (blue), we observe genes immune activation and inflammatory responses (e.g.
Il18rap
,
Tnfaip2

(
Lin et al. 2022
)
).
The third (violet) contains genes related to TCR activation signaling and Lymphocyte proliferation, such as
Zap70
,
Cd247
,
Cd28
and
Top2a
.
Finally, the fourth cluster (orange) has a distinct profile with an emphasis on metabolic enzymes alongside immune regulators and effector molecules (e.g.
Mthfd1l
and
Ahcy

(
Ducker & Rabinowitz 2017
)
).

Figure 2
:
Common gene co-expression network obtained using
GEPHI

(
Bastian et al. 2009
)
among 392 genes. Nodes (edges) represent genes (positive dependencies). Node size is proportional to the node degree. Nodes are divided into four main clusters based on their connections.
